## Standing context

### Deferred

Directive for the non-spec loop. Each item below is still owed by the non-spec staging, and none is authored. The loop authors it, or records an owner for each one it declines. The remedy of 2026-10-17 discharged the earlier items on the docs deliverable (DOCS-1), the tier-11 gate retargets (DOCS-1), the step dependency of CODE-1 on SPEC-2, the CRD phase comment (CODE-8), the CREATE-loser tests, the disposition-table tests (the Part A recycle axis; per-row intents for rows that match the tree stay declined, as non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1 decided), row D10 (REC-1 item 5), the seal-loss filing (REC-1 item 4), and the edge-case corrections (reconcile pass).

Trap: SPEC-1h names no set and SPEC-1g is a diagram label, so a blanket "describes a set" assertion is false for both.
2. **Redis-restart departures.** REC-1 records, or a sentence in `docs/runbooks/redis-failure.md`, for the two Redis-restart departures in spec-changes **Edge cases and accepted failure modes**; the post-restart cause (a release before the post-restart rehydration leaves the claim to the GC) in the `SandboxClaimOrphanRateHigh` runbook; and TEST-1 tier-8 cases asserting both recorded outcomes.

### [remedy.mechanism-table.2026-10-16]
DECISION (2026-10-16): Converged the SandboxClaim end-of-occupancy disposition as one code-derived table and regenerated SPEC-2 from it — BECAUSE the spec loop stopped after round 2 with the introspection verdict `redesign`: the disposition had been repaired one facet per round (round 1 found missing sites and a wrong predicate; its fixes added an acquisition-undo universal that let the CREATE loser delete the winner's claim and a "the pod retires" clause that the §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** contradicts; round 2 found the §6.2 Recycle edges header and §28.5.1 unstaged), and §29.4 step 17 was still unstaged, §6.2 **Pre-attached failure retry policy:** had been dropped with no recorded exclusion, and the predicate drifted between "ends the occupancy without reuse" and "an occupancy the pool does not reuse". What changed: non-spec-changes.md gains **SandboxClaim end-of-occupancy disposition table** (rows D1 to D18b over pool kind, `recycle.enabled`, pre-`running` or `running`, and outcome, each verified against 85c9ab19a), its invariants, the paths outside it, and the races it does not close; SPEC-2 gains a **Site classification.** list (home, edits, pointers, and out-of-scope sites with owners, including §5.2's failure-retire sentence and §6.2 **Pre-attached failure retry policy:** with owner 0085); SPEC-2a, 2b, 2c, 2e, 2f, 2g, and 2h use the one predicate wording; SPEC-2e now qualifies the existing **Recycle lifecycle** sentence instead of inserting a reason clause that was false on the `reclaimOnFailure` path; SPEC-2h's **Concurrent pod lifecycle** edit and SPEC-2j, SPEC-2k, and SPEC-2l state no pod outcome; SPEC-2j (§29.4 step 17), SPEC-2k (§29.4 step 12), and SPEC-2l (§7.2 snapshot-close step 3) are new; CODE-2 gains item 7 (`rollbackBinding` clears `Recycle` for an unregistered concurrent binding, row D18b) because a row-verification pass found that path patched `recycling` for a pre-`running` slot; every anchor was re-checked against 85c9ab19a and matches its target file once. Decision 18 and the impacts table move full_revoke deny-list granularity (C3) to P5b after the owner's P5 split (0094 D20), and the P5b row records that CODE-7 step 1 returns a pool lease whose credential the same step deny-lists. The landed F-4.6.30 chart fix (merge 638fc3855) bounds GC-backed departures by `claimOrphanTimeout` plus one sweep interval in REC-1, TEST-1, and the edge cases — ALTERNATIVES: a third facet-by-facet spec round (rejected: each round's fixes supplied the next round's findings); keeping SPEC-2e's inserted reason sentence (a second, partly false statement of the pre-`running` clause).
WATCHOUT: the disposition table is the only rule site for which gateway release deletes, patches, or leaves the per-pod claim. A later change to that disposition starts at the table, re-derives the affected rows from the tree, and only then edits SPEC-2c and the pointer sites. Do not add an acquisition-undo universal (row D8 must leave the CREATE winner's claim intact), and do not state a pod outcome at a releasing site.

DECISION: CRD phase comment (backlog item 2). CODE-8 item 1 edits the `SandboxClaimStatus.Phase` comment and the `WriteDispositionStatus` doc comment, runs `make generate`, and copies the regenerated CRD to `pkg/embedded/crds/` — BECAUSE `pkg/embedded/crds/crds_test.go` requires the embedded copy to be byte-identical to the chart manifest, and `bindingstate.go` repeats the same retired `failed` meaning — ALTERNATIVES: the type comment alone (leaves the embedded copy drifted and the writer's comment false).
DECISION: Force-terminate comment (backlog item 3). CODE-8 item 2 rewrites "the assigned pod is released to the pool" to the SPEC-2s pointer wording — ALTERNATIVES: none considered; the sentence restates the row SPEC-2s rewrites.
DECISION: Proto comment (backlog item 4). CODE-8 item 3 rewrites the `ShutdownRequest.recycle` comment and the matching `ShutdownRecycle` doc comment to point at §4.6.3 and §4.6.1, with `make generate-proto` — BECAUSE "the pod is replaced" was false before this proposal for a concurrent slot release with live siblings and is false for a release before the pod projects `claimed`, and SPEC-2m removes the spec sentence the comment mirrored; the proto comment is read by adapter authors — ALTERNATIVES: keeping it (true only for the exclusive non-recycling case, as spec.4.review-edit-sites.1 and spec.7.review-client-surface.1 judged).
DECISION: Summary Decision 15 (backlog item 5) now evicts for the resume family too, and CODE-5 item 2 has one eviction branch for `running`, `input_required`, `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action`; `releaseEarlierBinding` leaves CODE-5's targets and the 0091 impact row, the CODE-5 tier-1 test asserts eviction on every stale row, and the TEST-1 Tier 4 bullet says "evicts" — BECAUSE the ledger's OPENs at non-spec.1.fix-G6.1 and non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1 establish that no same-replica binding survives a resume-family write (the funnel releases it under `slotAccountLocks` right after its edge write) and that the reachable stale binding lives on a replica that lost a running-state takeover, whose adopter's failure funnel released the pod; a failed-disposition release there repeats the adopter's release and sends a stale replica's `Shutdown` to a pod it no longer owns, which D6's rule already forbids — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the release (a duplicate release, harmless only through CODE-1's idempotence); a release gated on the adopter's state (no replica can read it).
DECISION: Row D9 (backlog item 6) takes no code change. **Races the table does not close** gains its race, and REC-1 item 6 files it as an accepted departure — BECAUSE `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 joins the empty-phase claim `claimIdle` has just created, so any by-name delete on the `Binder.Resume` path can drain a pod a sibling joined; a safe delete needs the slot set on the resume path, which Non-goals declines for N2, and the delete fails closed for isolation — ALTERNATIVES: skipping the delete on a concurrent pool and leaving the empty-status claim to the claim-orphan GC (the plain `Claimer` request carries no pool kind, so it adds a branch on a path the proposal does not otherwise touch).
DECISION: Retry jitter (backlog item 7). CODE-1 item 5's pause stays an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE with no jitter requirement — BECAUSE the gateway's go-redis client keeps its default per-command retries with jittered 8-512 ms backoff (no `MaxRetries` or backoff override under `cmd/` or `pkg/`), and each retried release starts from an independent session end, so replicas do not retry in step against a recovering Redis — ALTERNATIVES: requiring a jittered pause (a requirement with no observed failure behind it).
DECISION: CODE-2 Part A runs its exclusive column on both `recycle.enabled` settings, with no `recycling` patch as the discriminating assertion on a recycling exclusive pool (row D1). REC-1 item 6 excludes the row D10 Defects bullet, which item 5 files, closing the double filing the f3.cleanup WATCHOUT recorded.

DECISION: A release that finds the `rehydrated` flag absent rehydrates through the existing Counter.rehydrate path with its own session added to the seed, then removes it (SPEC-1a one sentence; CODE-1 releaseScript returns {-2,0}; Release gains `rehydrated`; seed/rehydrate take an optional extra member) — BECAUSE without it a post-restart last release removed nothing, skipped the disposition, and ClaimSlot Pass 1 (slotclaimer.go "Pass 1") reused the bound claim across the occupancy-zero boundary without the scrub/delete/retire, and GC predicate 1 never selects a claim an active session references — ALTERNATIVES: rehydrate-then-check "empty after removal" as a second disposition disjunct (fails open if the process exits between seed and SREM); dropping the `removed` gate (duplicate release re-patches a `reserved` claim to `recycling`); keeping the departure (regresses scrub-before-reuse under a store outage).
WATCHOUT: Do not have the release rehydrate without seeding its own member: at a post-terminal release the session row is terminal, so GetActiveSlotsByPod omits it and the release again removes nothing — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go Counter.rehydrate (seeds from source only), pgstore GetActiveSlotsByPod (non-terminal rows).
WATCHOUT: When a peer holds the rehydrating lock, the release waits for the flag (waitForFlag) and its retry finds no member, because the peer's seed did not include it. For two concurrent last releases this is safe (the seeding one removes its member and runs the disposition once); a later round must not "fix" it by seeding in waitForFlag — EVIDENCE: slotcounter.go Counter.rehydrate `if !acquired { return false, c.waitForFlag(ctx, podID) }`.
DECISION: SPEC-1a reservation sentences reordered so membership is checked first ("whatever the cardinality is"; "Otherwise ..."), matching CODE-1 reserveScript (SISMEMBER before the cap) — BECAUSE a present member at the cap had two outcomes; tier-1 row "including at the cap" is the discriminating assertion.
FACT: Today's releaseScript returns 0 on an absent key (`if current <= 0 then return 0`), so today's post-restart release does run the disposition; the staged set design had regressed that — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go releaseScript; slotclaimer.go ReleaseSlot `if remaining > 0`.
OPEN: The `pool` label for lenny_slot_rehydration_total on the release path is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE in CODE-1 item 3 (caller vs Sandbox warmpool.LabelPool label); SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot has no pool parameter today. A non-spec round may prefer to fix it.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c's §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row is the single home of the release-delete predicate: every exclusive release ends the occupancy, a concurrent release ends it at slot occupancy zero (§5.2 cited), and it ends without reuse when the pool does not recycle, the released slot is pre-`running` (§6.2 term, either concurrency), or on an exclusive pool the session failed or crashed; an undone acquisition or slot reservation is a pre-`running` release — BECAUSE the code deletes on the exclusive pre-running reclaim even on a recycling pool (reclaim.go ReclaimClaimed → deleteClaimDetached, no recycle check; Decision 5 keeps it) and Binder.Release drains on failed/crashed even with Recycle set (release.go `result.Recycle && disposition != dispositionFailed && !result.Crashed()`) — ALTERNATIVES: keying on "session never reached `running`" (a `ready` row can hold a registered binding that recycles), keying on "holds no adapter connection" (BindReservedSlot failure holds one and still deletes via recycle=false), patching the exclusive reclaim to `recycling` (no adapter connection to carry the whole-pod Shutdown).
DECISION: §4.6.1 **Reserved hold**, §6.2 **`reserved` hold semantics.**, §6.2 **Concurrent pod lifecycle**, and the SPEC-2f diagram note each cite §4.6.3 ("unless/when the release deletes the claim") instead of carrying an exception list — BECAUSE any list there is a second copy of SPEC-2c's predicate — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "an occupancy the pool reuses" wording (competes with "without reuse"; a recycle disposition that retires is not a reuse).
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2c's old clause "an acquisition that did not complete its slot reservation deletes the claim it created" described reserveSlot's unguarded fresh-claim `c.Client.Delete`, which drains a pod a sibling already reserved onto (Pass 1 accepts an empty-phase claim; only released/failed are terminal). Do not reintroduce it, and do not guard it with UID/resourceVersion preconditions: a sibling reservation never writes the claim object, so only the slot-set gate sees it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (writeBoundStatus, StampPodTenant, StampMaxPodUptime failure branches) and Pass 1 `!found || claimstate.IsTerminal(...)`; pkg/sandboxclaim/state/state.go IsTerminal
MISTAKE: SPEC-2c/2g used "rollback" as a separate claim-delete writer edge; it is not spec vocabulary and licensed the unguarded delete above. Removed from both; CODE-1 item 4 now routes the post-create rollbacks through `ReleaseSlot(rctx, sb.Name, req.SessionID, false, false)`.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2b's sentence "A recycling pool's release patches the claim to `recycling` instead" was deleted; do not restore an unconditional recycling statement in §4.6.1 — the failed/crashed exclusive release and the pre-running release delete on a recycling pool. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Reserved hold**; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go ReleaseSlotReservation passes recycle=false
FACT: §6.2 recycle-edges label listed "a failed session on a maxConcurrentSessions: 1 pool (see §5.2)" as a recycle-disposition retire; the code takes the drain path (claim delete, no recycling patch). SPEC-2h substitution 1 deletes it; the base `claimed ──→ draining (claim deletion)` edge covers the case. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md Recycle edges block; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go retire path

### [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: full_revoke deny-lists the leases of the user's non-terminal sessions after the SessionStore List and before the first cancel write; §11.4 step 1 is the one ordering home, step 6 states the lease return through the step-2 release or step-4 reclaim, the Note lists the deny-list first and keeps only the count sentence — BECAUSE no release path (coordination Sweeper, resume tail, post-terminal hook, fan-out) runs on a non-terminal row, so a deny-list write before the first cancel precedes every release — ALTERNATIVES: narrowing the Note to "releases it performs itself" plus a departure (leaves a race on a security path that one moved call removes); per-session deny-list inside the cancel loop (same window per session, no gain); deny-list before and after the cancels (second enumeration for a residual whose token dies at release).
WATCHOUT: do not restore an ordering guarantee such as "so no release precedes its leases' deny-list entries". A lease assigned after the step-1 enumeration has no entry; the residual is accepted because the release removes the lease record and the proxy then rejects the token (LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID). It is deliberately not staged. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:468 (`s.leases.Remove(leaseID)`); cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:185 (`LeasesBySession`).
WATCHOUT: do not renumber §11.4's steps. interactionstore code cites step 7 and sits outside CODE-7. — EVIDENCE: grep '11.4[^0-9]*step 7' pkg/
FACT: the cancel writes commit sequentially in `terminateUserSessions` before `runFullRevokeFanOut` runs; today `RevokeUserLeases` is called from the fan-out after the pod step. Once a row is terminal, `sweepTerminal` hands it to `ReleaseTerminalBinding` on the replica that binds it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:463-500, :563-577; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_invalidate.go:145-160; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:532-533; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:646.
FACT: SPEC-3b's widened first anchor ("it cancels the user's non-terminal sessions ... revokes the user's credential leases,") and the SPEC-3a step-1/step-6 anchors each match spec/11_policy-and-controls.md exactly once (checked by script).
OPEN: the summary's 0094 impact row says 0094's draft "runs the deny-list step before any release" inside the fan-out; that describes 0094's text and its action (re-baseline on CODE-7) stays right, but the 0094 re-baseline must move its deny-list call into `terminateUserSessions` ahead of the cancel writes.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a release that finds the `rehydrated` flag absent rehydrates the set with its own session's member added to the seed, then removes it; the disposition predicate ("removed the member and left the set empty") stays the one rule — BECAUSE the session row is already terminal at a post-terminal release, so a plain rehydrate-then-SREM removes nothing and skips the disposition again, and Pass 1 (slotclaimer.go ClaimSlot) then reuses the still-`bound` pod without the occupancy-zero scrub/delete — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "disposition when the rehydrated set is empty" adds a second disjunct to the predicate (hair) and, if the process dies between seed and SREM, a retried release finds the flag set and skips the disposition (fails open); seeding the releaser instead leaves a phantom member that over-counts (fails closed) until another release path removes it.
DECISION: leaked-slot lifetime keeps its single home in §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.**; SPEC-1a's "A `leaked` member stays counted until ..." sentence is deleted and the Redis-restart loss stays a recorded departure (spec-changes Edge cases) — BECAUSE owner preference is residual gaps as bounded departures rather than new spec rules, and the loss predates the proposal (today's seed from rows drops it too) — ALTERNATIVES: rewording §6.2/§5.2 Slot cleanup/Slot (session mode) to cite SPEC-1a codifies a weaker guarantee in the spec and pulls docs/reference/state-machines.md and glossary.md into the edit.
DECISION: register-vocabulary scope rule for REG-SLOTCOUNT restatements: text that DEFINES the register as an atomic counter or describes increment/decrement/INCR/reset-to-0 is edited (grep `atomic[^.]*counter` near slot, plus the §6.2 diagram labels); nominal labels "slot counter", "slot-counter keys", "Redis-counter occupancy" stay — BECAUSE the set's cardinality still counts slots and SPEC-1g itself keeps "Redis-counter occupancy" — ALTERNATIVES: a global vocabulary sweep (spec/04 §4.6.1, spec/05:587, spec/06:132/135/137/166, spec/12:202, spec/18:532, docs/reference/state-machines.md:258/268) is a separate decision, not owed by finding 0.
FACT: today's leaked release skips the counter entirely and returns (false,nil) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go ReleaseSlot `if leaked { return false, nil }`; today's releaseScript returns 0 on an absent key, so the post-restart release runs the disposition (deletes the claim under live siblings) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go releaseScript `if current <= 0 then return 0`.
FACT: `lenny_slot_rehydration_total` is emitted only from reserveSlot via SlotClaimer.OnRehydrate(podID, pool); a release that performs the seed must report it too or the per-restart event is lost for that pod — EVIDENCE: slotclaimer.go reserveSlot `if rehydrated {` and recordRehydration.
FACT: the Redis outage path does not clear the `rehydrated` flag on recovery (clearOutage only resets the outage clock), so "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt through the standard blocking-rehydration path" (§12.4 failure table) holds only when Redis restarted — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go clearOutage.
OPEN: a reservation admitted by the §12.4 Postgres fallback adds no member; when Redis kept its data, that session is uncounted and, if it outlives its siblings, the last sibling's release empties the set and runs the disposition under it (and its own release then removes nothing). Pre-existing in the proposal (non-spec Edge case "A reservation made during a Redis outage"); a later round should file it.
WATCHOUT: seeding the releaser means a duplicate release that crosses a Redis restart (first release ran the disposition before the restart) can re-run the disposition: WriteRecyclingStatus has no `reserved` guard and would re-patch a held claim to `recycling` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/bindingstate.go WriteRecyclingStatus (only short-circuits on `recycling`). It fails closed (re-scrub or retire); the reviewer's alternative has the same exposure.
WATCHOUT: spec/29 is hard-wrapped (~105 cols, 3-space indent in §29.2 numbered steps, 2-space in §29.10 bullets); staged anchors at step 8 and the §29.10 occupancy bullet must quote the wrapped text exactly, line breaks included — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.2 step 8 ("checks the\n   counter and increments"), §29.10 ("ceilings\n  concurrent slots").
WATCHOUT: spec-changes Edge case "A reseed drops leaked members" also names the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset as a loss, but that reset runs for a replacement pod (Counter.Reset doc: "pod-replacement path"), which §6.2's "until pod termination" already permits; left as is (separate, minor).

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c's §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row is the one home of "which release deletes the claim". Predicate: every exclusive release ends the occupancy, a concurrent one ends it at slot occupancy zero (§5.2); it ends without reuse when the pool does not recycle, the released slot is pre-`running` (§6.2 term, either concurrency), or, on maxConcurrentSessions: 1, the session failed or crashed; the undo of an acquisition or slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules — BECAUSE this one wording covers the exclusive recycling pre-running reclaim (F2), retires the non-spec word "rollback", and puts reserveSlot's fresh-claim delete under the SPEC-1a occupancy-zero gate (F4) — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the rollback clause outright (leaves reserveSlot's unconditional delete as an unnamed writer edge); per-site exception lists in §4.6.1/§6.2 (hair).
DECISION: every other site (§4.6.1 Reserved hold, §6.2 reserved hold semantics, §6.2 Concurrent pod lifecycle, SPEC-2f note) qualifies its recycling sentence with "unless/when the release deletes the claim ([§4.6.3])" rather than restating the predicate. SPEC-2e keeps its sentence with "slot" dropped, because it carries the reason clause (no adapter connection for the recycle Shutdown).
FACT: the gateway's claim-delete discriminator in code is which release function runs: ReclaimClaimed / ReleaseSlotReservation (recycle=false) delete; Binder.Release / Binder.ReleaseSlot recycle. A ready row can hold a registered binding between registerBinding and the running commit (start.go registerBinding precedes the commit), but the adapter has already recorded the session then, so §6.2's "pre-`running`" (adapter record step) still matches the code there. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go registerBinding call; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go terminalReclaimPreRunning; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go Release recycle branch
UNVERIFIED: a preConnect pod "attached at finalize" (terminalReclaimPreRunning comment) gives a pre-running session a live BindResult; such a release recycles in code while SPEC-2c's pre-`running` predicate says delete. A mechanism-lens reviewer should check whether that attach exists and whether the adapter has recorded the slot by then.
WATCHOUT: reserveSlot's fresh-claim rollback delete is unguarded today; a peer's ClaimSlot Pass 1 accepts a claim with an empty phase (only released/failed are terminal), so a sibling can join between CreateClaim and writeBoundStatus. The CODE-1 item 4 fix routes the post-create rollbacks through ReleaseSlot keyed by session. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (writeBoundStatus/StampPodTenant/StampMaxPodUptime rollbacks), Pass 1 IsTerminal skip; pkg/sandboxclaim/state/state.go IsTerminal
DEFERRED [0093_fix_release-ownership-for-pods-concurrent-workspace-slots-per-po.implementation-checklist.md]: S4 (CODE-1) lists "Depends on: S1", but CODE-1 item 4 now implements SPEC-2c's rule that a reservation undo is a pre-`running` release, so S4 must also depend on S2.
OPEN: docs/reference/state-machines.md (Recycle transitions intro, the `claimed → draining` row naming "a failed session on a pool with maxConcurrentSessions: 1", and the concurrent paragraph) mirrors §6.2 text that SPEC-2c/2d/2e already change. non-spec-changes **Staged docs changes** says no reader-facing page changes. A later round should stage a docs edit, which also needs a checklist step.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: full_revoke's deny-list call moves ahead of the cancel writes, into `terminateUserSessions` between its `List` and its first `Update`; §11.4 step 1 is the ordering home, step 6 becomes "each lease returns to its pool through its session's release (step 2) or reclaim (step 4)", and the SPEC-3b Note ordering sentence is deleted, keeping only the count sentence — BECAUSE once a row is terminal the coordination Sweeper (`sweepTerminal` -> `ReleaseTerminalBinding`, any replica that binds it, 15s default) and the resume tail release it, and the release removes lease records so `LeasesBySession` skips them — ALTERNATIVES: (b) narrow the Note to "the releases it performs itself" plus a departure (rejected: owner prefers fail-closed on security paths, and (a) costs one moved call); deny-list inside step 6 with "before step 2" (rejected: the numbered list would no longer read in execution order, which SPEC-3a's own step-2 edit relies on); deleting step 6 (rejected: renumbers step 7, which interactionstore.go and interactionstore/pgstore cite).
DECISION: the staged text carries no "so no release precedes its deny-list entry" guarantee. A lease assigned after the step-1 enumeration (an in-flight start, a fallback rotation) gets no deny-list entry; its token stops resolving when its session's release removes the record, because the proxy answers LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID for a token with no lease record. Accepted residual, recorded here rather than staged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go (GetByToken miss -> LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID).
FACT: today's order is cancel writes (users.go terminateUserSessions) then `runFullRevokeFanOut` (users_invalidate.go: pod terminate, then RevokeUserLeases, then tokens); RevokeUserLeases deny-lists `CredentialKey()` and calls `Remove` — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go RevokeUserLeases; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go sweepTerminal.
FACT: §11.4 step numbers are cited in code: step 1 (sessionstore memstore/pgstore List filter), step 2 (propagator.go, billing_security_workers.go), step 6 (user_revocation.go deny-list marker comments), step 7 (interactionstore.go, interactionstore/pgstore). Renumbering breaks step-7 citers outside CODE-7's touched files.
WATCHOUT: after this fix user_revocation.go's "§11.4 step 6" deny-list citations must become step 1; CODE-7 item 8 names this.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The slot set adds no etcd write and no new hot key; Redis ops per session stay one reserve script and one release script (SADD/SREM/SCARD are O(1)); set memory is at most maxConcurrentSessions plus leaked members per pod. WATCHOUT: The slot-counter code never rebuilds on Redis recovery without data loss (it rehydrates only when the flag is absent), although §12.4 says "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt". ### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: A releasing site states only whether the release deletes the claim (SPEC-2c is the home); the pod's resulting phase is §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** and is never restated at a release site. SPEC-2e lost "and the pod retires", SPEC-2f lost "in which case the pod drains", the spec-changes Edge-cases bullet "Pre-running release on a recycling pool" was deleted, and summary row 0079 lost "retires the pod" — BECAUSE a claim deleted before any binding state, or while the pod projects `reserved`, leaves the pod `idle` — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "rather than patching it to `recycling`" (X-not-Y) and "a pod that projects `claimed` drains" (copy of §4.6.1); a per-binding-state outcome table (copy of §4.6.1).
WATCHOUT: Do not write a pod outcome ("retires", "drains", "not reused") next to a claim delete anywhere in the staging. The projection decides: no binding state leaves the warm-fill phase; deleted while `reserved` returns `idle`; deleted while `claimed` drains — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go (default branch "Leave the warm-fill phase untouched"); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 projection bullets ("`idle` for a pod in a warm-inventory phase with no claim", "`idle` when the claim is deleted while the pod projects `reserved`").
DECISION: SPEC-2c's universal "The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules" was replaced by "A slot reservation withdrawn after its claim `CREATE` fails (§4.6.1) ends no occupancy" — BECAUSE the post-CREATE rollbacks (writeBoundStatus, StampPodTenant, StampMaxPodUptime) already fall under SPEC-2c's "released slot is pre-`running`" clause, and the only act the universal got wrong was the CREATE loser, whose set-empty withdrawal would otherwise delete the winner's `recycling`/`reserved` claim — ALTERNATIVES: appending ", except that an acquisition whose claim CREATE failed deletes no claim" (narrows a failed universal); scoping to "after its claim CREATE succeeded" (misses Pass 1 joins of an existing claim); deleting with nothing added (SPEC-1a calls every keyed removal "a release", so the loser still meets "the release that brings the slot occupancy to zero ends it"); reordering reserveSlot to CREATE first (hot-path change; tree is already right).
WATCHOUT: Do not restore any universal covering "every acquisition undo". The CREATE-failure withdrawal is a raw Counter.Release with no disposition, and must stay so — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (Counter.Reserve before CreateClaim; CreateClaim error branch calls only c.Counter.Release); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Pod claim mechanism** "every other replica receives an `AlreadyExists` conflict and retries with a different idle pod".
DECISION: §6.2 Recycle edges header now reads "occupancy reaches zero and the release does not delete the claim under §4.6.3" (SPEC-2h first substitution); "after clean session termination" was removed rather than kept beside the pointer — BECAUSE "clean" was a partial copy of SPEC-2c's predicate that excluded a concurrent pool's failed last release, which recycles (slotbinder.go Binder.ReleaseSlot has no failure gate) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's additive wording (two predicate copies whose conjunction contradicts §4.6.3 on concurrent pools).
DECISION: New SPEC-2i deletes "with the `failed` disposition" from §28.5.1 `CH-ATTACH` **Degradation.** and keeps its §6.2 "Pod crash during an active session" citation — BECAUSE SPEC-2d narrows `failed` to scrub-failure retirement, and the code's crashed exclusive release deletes the claim (podsession/release.go drain → podclaim.DeleteClaim) — ALTERNATIVES: "released and its claim deleted (§4.6.3)" (a third statement of SPEC-2c's rule).
FACT: The §28.5.1 anchor lines and the §6.2 Recycle edges header anchor each match the tree once (grep -c), and the new header lines are no wider than the old 103-column first line — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.1 (heading "#### 28.5.1 Gateway-to-pod", **Degradation.** bullet); spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "Recycle edges (recycle.enabled: true; ..." line.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0093/proposals/0093_fix_release-ownership-for-pods-concurrent-workspace-slots-per-po/0093_fix_release-ownership-for-pods-concurrent-workspace-slots-per-po.implementation-checklist.md]: S2 ("all of SPEC-2") now also lands SPEC-2i into spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.1; S2's account stays true, but any S2 doc-reconciliation or spec-file list must include §28.5.1.
OPEN: non-spec lane coverage gap (not falsified, not authored here): no test asserts that a CreateClaim AlreadyExists/Forbidden withdrawal that empties the set leaves the winner's `recycling` or `reserved` claim intact; that is the case that discriminates SPEC-2c's new withdrawal sentence (non-spec-changes.md Tier 1 podclaim row on reserveSlot rollbacks).
OPEN: docs/reference/state-machines.md mirrors the §6.2 Recycle edges header ("Occupancy zero, claim patched `bound → recycling`") and now also needs the §4.6.3 qualification; this extends the docs deliverable already owed per spec.1.fix-G2.1's DEFERRED.

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: full_revoke returns each lease at §11.4 step 1: `RevokeUserLeases` deny-lists the credential and then calls the credential assigner's `Release(leaseID)` in place of the shipped bare `Remove`; step 6 becomes the pointer "Step 1 revokes the credential leases held by those sessions." — BECAUSE deleting the shared Postgres lease row makes every replica's LLM proxy miss on `GetByToken` and answer LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID, independent of the Redis pub/sub deny-list publish (the §4.9 startup rebuild restores only `status='revoked'` credentials, so a lost publish or a restarted replica is never repaired), and `Release` also returns the pool slot, so a binding-replica exit before its release no longer strands it — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUT below.
WATCHOUT: do not return to a deny-list-only `RevokeUserLeases` with the lease return left to the session release (round-1 staging). Under a Redis reset, peer proxies accept the token until the coordination Sweeper releases the row (15s default), and if the binding replica exits before its release, the token stays valid until ExpiresAt. The shipped design had a zero window. Also rejected: a revoked mark on the lease record plus a new §4.9 proxy rule (a new field inside the sealed blob, which renewal's `Advance` rewrites; it still leaks the slot on a binding-replica exit; it contradicts summary Decision 1's no-new-field claim), and restoring the bare `Remove` (it strands the pool slot, which is the F-11.4.10 slot-return half). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go (GetByToken miss -> LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID); cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy.go buildLLMProxy passes w.llmLeases (Postgres store when pgPool is set, executor_credentials.go); pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go Sweeper Interval default.
MISTAKE: round 1 (spec.1.fix-G3) moved the lease return off `RevokeUserLeases` onto the session release, and summary Decision 8 kept "only the deny-list write". That weakened a security control below the shipped fleet-wide durable rejection. The finding cost a round.
FACT: both `Release` implementations are keyed by lease ID and do nothing for an unknown ID (credassign.go Service.Release -> releaseLocked `GetByID` miss returns; client.go Client.Release `GetByID` miss returns nil). The Token Service answers a duplicate revoke with NotFound, which the client treats as clean. So the session's later `ReleaseThrough`/`ReleaseSession` finds no lease and does nothing, and step 1's call is the one real release. `credassign.Assigner` already exposes `Release(leaseID string) error`, and `w.credAssign` is built in buildStores (stores.go -> buildExecutorAndCredentials) before buildAdminRouter (main.go). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go Service.Release/releaseLocked; client.go Client.Release; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go call order.
WATCHOUT: with the in-process `credassign.Service` (dev mode, no --token-service-grpc-addr), pool `active` counts are per replica, so a step-1 `Release` on the handling replica decrements its own count rather than the minter's. This is the moved-out N4 per-replica accounting defect (problem-statement Scope), not this finding. Do not stage a fix here. — EVIDENCE: credassign.go releaseLocked `ps.active[...]--` guarded by `> 0`.
DECISION: the tier-9 wording became "deny-lists and returns every lease ... before its first cancel write" rather than requiring all deny-list writes before any return — BECAUSE the deny entry is per credential and the row deletion is per lease, so ordering across leases buys nothing; a per-lease deny-then-release loop is what CODE-7 item 7 builds. The new tier-8 case (non-binding replica, dropped deny-list publish) is the assertion that separates this design from the deny-list-only one; the tier-1/4/9 rows pass under both.
FACT: the count sentence "The reported lease count is the number of leases step 1 adds to the deny list." stays true (every enumerated lease is deny-listed, and a `Release` error still counts it per CODE-7 item 7).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c's undo universal ("The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules") is deleted and replaced by one sentence saying a slot reservation withdrawn after a failed claim `CREATE` ends no occupancy — BECAUSE every post-CREATE undo (reserveSlot's writeBoundStatus/StampPodTenant/StampMaxPodUptime rollbacks) is already a release of a slot §6.2's `idle ──→ claimed` label counts as assigned (CREATE won and the reservation succeeded) and that is pre-`running`, so the base predicate covers them; only the CREATE loser, which SPEC-1a's script vocabulary still calls a release, needed a statement — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "except that an acquisition whose claim CREATE failed deletes no claim" (narrows the round-1 universal, same kind; the measured failure pattern); "undo after its CREATE succeeded" (also needs "or on an existing claim" for Pass 1 joins: an enumeration that grows); reordering reserveSlot to CREATE before Reserve (code churn, and the post-CREATE reserve failure still needs the keyed release).
WATCHOUT: deleting the universal leaves the exclusive Claimer.claimIdle fresh-claim delete after a writeBoundStatus failure unnamed as such; it is a release of a pre-`running` exclusive occupancy under the base predicate (and the tree row never named it). Do not re-add an undo universal to cover it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go claimIdle (`_ = c.Client.Delete(ctx, claim)` after writeBoundStatus).
FACT: the CREATE loser's withdrawal is today a raw `c.Counter.Release` with no disposition, and CODE-1 item 4 keeps it raw — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (`CreateClaim` error branch).
DECISION: a releasing site never states the pod's resulting phase; §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** owns it. SPEC-2e drops "and the pod retires", SPEC-2f drops "in which case the pod drains", the spec-changes Edge-case bullet "Pre-running release on a recycling pool" is deleted, and the summary 0079 impact row drops "and retires the pod" — BECAUSE a delete of a claim with no binding state leaves the pod idle (occupancy.go "Leave the warm-fill phase untouched") and a delete while the pod projects `reserved` returns it to idle; both pods are clean, so the outcome is safe but the sentences were false — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "and a pod that projects `claimed` drains" (restates §4.6.1 at a second site).
FACT: a failed `reserved → bound` rebind (WriteRebindStatus error) deletes nothing in either claimer; the rebind-then-reserve-failure path is a keyed slot release on a `bound` claim — EVIDENCE: claimer.go rebindReserved, slotclaimer.go rebindReservedSlot.
DECISION: the §6.2 Recycle edges header replaces "after clean session termination" with the §4.6.3 pointer rather than adding the pointer beside it — BECAUSE "clean session termination" is a partial copy of SPEC-2c's failed/crashed clause and is wrong for a concurrent pool, whose failed last running release recycles (SPEC-2c's failed/crashed clause is exclusive-only; slotbinder.go Binder.ReleaseSlot has no failure gate) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's additive wording keeps both, which excludes the concurrent failed-last case §4.6.3 recycles.
DECISION: §28.5.1 **Degradation.** deletes "with the `failed` disposition" and keeps its existing §6.2 "Pod crash during an active session" citation as the home of the outcome — BECAUSE pointing beats restating SPEC-2c's delete rule — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "released and its claim deleted (§4.6.3)" restates the rule.
OPEN: no test pins the CREATE-loser case (a withdrawal that empties the set leaves the winner's `recycling` or `reserved` claim intact). The non-spec Tier 1 `podclaim` row's "deleted only when the set is then empty" stays true but does not discriminate; the non-spec lane should add the row.
WATCHOUT: docs/reference/state-machines.md Recycle transitions intro and the `claimed → claimed` "Occupancy zero, claim patched bound → recycling" row mirror the §6.2 header; the docs deliverable owed since spec.1 G2 must also take the §4.6.3 qualification — EVIDENCE: docs/reference/state-machines.md "### Recycle transitions".

### [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: full_revoke returns each lease at §11.4 step 1: `RevokeUserLeases` adds the credential to the deny list, then calls the credential assigner's `Release(leaseID)` (consumer interface over `w.credAssign`); `userLeaseStore` drops `Remove`; step 6 becomes a pointer to step 1 — BECAUSE `Release` deletes the Postgres lease row (proxy `GetByToken` miss -> LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID on every replica, no pub/sub dependency) and returns the pool slot (Token Service `RevokeCredentials` -> `assign.Release`), closing both the Redis-reset window and the binding-replica-exit leak with no new field, column, or proxy rule — ALTERNATIVES: a revoked mark on the lease record plus a proxy check (new lease field or column, a new §4.9 matching rule, a lost-update hazard against renewal's `Advance` that rewrites the whole blob, and the slot still leaks when the binding replica dies); keeping the deny-list-only design as a departure (degrades the shipped zero-window revocation; conflicts with owner fail-closed preference); restoring the shipped bare `Remove` (durable, but strands the pool slot, which is the F-11.4.10 slot-return defect).
CORRECTS [summary.md alternatives "Returning leases from `RevokeUserLeases` through `credassign.Release`"]: the stated costs do not hold. Both `Release` implementations are keyed by lease ID and no-op on a missing record (credassign.go `releaseLocked` GetByID miss; client.go `Release` GetByID miss), the Token Service answers a second revoke NotFound which the client treats as clean, and no session release runs on a non-terminal row, so step 1's `Release` is the only one and the session's later `ReleaseThrough` lists no lease.
FACT: the LLM proxy reads the lease row straight from Postgres per request (pgstore `GetByToken`, no cache) and rejects a miss with LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID before the deny-list check — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:176-181; pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:322-332
FACT: renewal writes leases only through `Advance`, which updates and inserts none, so a lease row deleted at step 1 is not resurrected by a peer's renewal worker — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:334-360; pgstore.go:228-280
FACT: production gateways use `credassign.Client` (Token Service, central slot accounting); the in-process `credassign.Service` with per-replica `ps.active` is the §17.4 dev profile only — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:129-149; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:498
WATCHOUT: `credassign.Client.Release` removes the lease record even when the revoke RPC fails (breaker open), so the token dies but the Token Service slot may not return; this is the pre-existing behavior of every Client release, not a full_revoke regression, and is not staged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-376
WATCHOUT: in a multi-replica in-process (dev) deployment, step 1's `Release` on the handling replica decrements that replica's `ps.active`, not the assigning replica's; this is the per-replica credential-accounting class (N4) the problem statement moved out — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:458-470
WATCHOUT: the tier-9 line said the deny-list writes precede "any" lease return; a per-lease loop (deny, release, deny, release) violates that wording. The redesign restates the tier-9 assertion as "deny-lists and returns every lease before the first cancel write"; do not impose a two-pass loop to satisfy the old wording.

### [spec.2.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The occupancy projection decides what a claim DELETE does to the pod from the phase the controller last projected, not from the gateway's intent. A delete while the pod projects `claimed` drains it. A delete while it projects `reserved` returns it to idle. A delete of a claim that never got a binding state (the projection leaves the warm-fill phase untouched) leaves the pod `idle`. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:410-416 (projection bullets); pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:99-140
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2c now counts the undo of an acquisition or slot reservation as a pre-`running` release. reserveSlot's writeBoundStatus-failure undo deletes a claim that has no binding state. On a recycling pool, SPEC-2e's "and the pod retires" and the edge-case bullet "The pod is not reused" are therefore false on that path, because the pod stays idle. Retirement is the projection's decision. A gateway-side sentence should say only that the claim is deleted rather than patched to `recycling`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:720-724
UNVERIFIED: On a concurrent pool, a slot release empties the set (SREM) and then deletes the claim by name. A sibling reservation that lands between the two joins the existing claim without writing to it, so no UID/resourceVersion precondition can detect it. This is a Redis-to-apiserver TOCTOU that predates this proposal, and no edge-case bullet names it. Owner: a later full-pool reviewer should decide whether it belongs in the departures.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: A podRegistry binding exists only from the §4.7.1 record step onward, which is where §6.2 says a slot reaches `running`. So D6 (`ReleaseTerminalBinding`) and the hook's executor release never release a pre-`running` slot, and SPEC-2c's "released slot is pre-`running`" clause does not reach them. An exclusive recycling D6 recycle is therefore not a contradiction of SPEC-2c. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:66-78; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "Pre-`running` slot cleanup." paragraph
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2c's undo clause ("The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules") drops r1's "the claim it created" bound. CODE-1 item 4 keeps the failed-CreateClaim rollback as a raw `Counter.Release` with no disposition, which is correct because the existing claim belongs to the CREATE winner. Keep the undo clause scoped to a claim the undoing path created or reserved onto. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:220; non-spec-changes.md:70; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:704-716
FACT: Counter.Reset deletes active_slots, rehydrated, and rehydrating together, so a §10.1.4 whole-pod reset re-arms rehydration on the next reservation or release. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482-488
FACT: WriteRecyclingStatus has no phase guard beyond the recycling no-op, so the edge-case bullet's reserved→recycling patch on a duplicate post-restart release applies as stated. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/bindingstate.go:92-110

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: In production the LLM proxy resolves a lease token against the shared Postgres lease store (GetByToken, LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID on a miss), so the shipped full_revoke `Remove` (a Postgres DELETE) invalidates the revoked user's leases on every replica independent of Redis pub/sub — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:322-327; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74-83; cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy.go:141; pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:282-285; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-190
FACT: full_revoke deny-list entries are in-memory, propagated only by pub/sub, and are not covered by the §4.9 startup rebuild query (it rebuilds only credentials with status='revoked') — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1792-1804
FACT: the coordination Sweeper releases a terminal row this replica binds once per interval (default 15s), which is the durable fallback for a lost pub/sub release request — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:243-249, 428-433, 530-533
FACT: Counter.rehydrate re-checks the rehydrated flag under both the in-process mutex and the SET NX lock and returns without seeding when it is set, so a release-path rehydration adds its own member only when it is the seeder; at most one GetActiveSlotsByPod per pod per Redis restart still holds with release-path rehydration — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:326-395
WATCHOUT: RevokeUserLeases' Deny write (`Propagator.Revoke`) returns no error, so SPEC-3a step 1's deny-list-before-cancel order cannot block the cancel writes on a Redis failure — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:162-165,184-190

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Counter.rehydrate re-checks the `rehydrated` flag under both the in-process mutex and the SET NX lock, so a release-path rehydration (SPEC-1a) that loses the race to a peer's seed does not seed its own member; its retried release script then removes nothing and skips the disposition, and the peer's removing release runs it once. Release-path seeding therefore adds no double disposition outside the recorded Redis-restart departures. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:326-395
FACT: The occupancy projection drains a pod whose claim is deleted while it projects `claimed`, and returns it to `idle` only when the claim is deleted from `reserved`. So SPEC-2c's claim delete for a failed mcs1 session or a pre-running release fails closed, and the duplicate-release departure's "deletes the held claim" returns an already scrubbed pod to idle. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:415-416
DECISION: did not file the §12.4 "On Redis recovery, the slot set is rebuilt" sentence (SPEC-1d) — BECAUSE that sentence is correct as spec intent, and the gap is in code (clearOutage never clears the flag after an outage with no restart). Its remedy is code or the non-spec departure, which is outside the spec loop. This round-2 reliability pass agrees with spec.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT — ALTERNATIVES: a new spec rule that clears the flag of fallback-admitted pods on recovery; rejected because the owner prefers bounded departures to new rules, and the fix is code-side.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: the go-redis re-send and clearOutage FACTs saved re-deriving the recovery paths.
OPEN: spec.1.review-reliability.1's OPEN (the sibling's last release runs the disposition under a fallback-admitted session after an outage in which Redis kept its data) is still unresolved in the non-spec Edge case "A reservation made during a Redis outage". That case states only "the session's release finds no member and changes nothing" and omits the disposition-under-live-session consequence. The between-loops pass or the non-spec loop owns it.

UNVERIFIED: §29.4 step 17 says "On the default disposition the claim is deleted" at every session end with no occupancy qualifier, while SPEC-2c deletes on a concurrent pool only at occupancy zero. §29 traces run under the concurrent condition (spec/29:17-20). ### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c's no-reuse clause keys on gateway publication ("when no gateway replica published the session's binding to the pod", citing §7.3) instead of §6.2 pre-`running` — BECAUSE §6.2 (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "Pre-`running` slot cleanup.") and §4.7.1 put `running` at the adapter's record, which precedes `registerBinding` and which the gateway cannot observe after an unknown start-RPC outcome; publication (`publishBinding`, called by `registerBinding`, create_and_start.go, delegated_child_materialize.go, resume_driver.go after the resume commit) is what every table row already used — ALTERNATIVES: reclassify D18/D18b/D14/D15 as `running` and make D18b recycle (needs recycle code on ReleaseUncommitted and Launch failures; predicate uncomputable at those sites); key on the `running` commit (diverges when registerBinding succeeds and commitStart is superseded, D3b/D4b recycle).
FACT: the disposition table's fifth column is now **Binding** (`published`/`unpublished`); every row kept its classification. Old invariant 3 is deleted and invariants renumber 1-5. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_registration.go `registerBinding` → `publishBinding`; start.go registerBinding failure → `rollbackBinding`.
WATCHOUT: "pre-`running`" still appears in its §6.2/session-state sense (summary decisions 5, 9, 11, 19, 66, 70; `terminalReclaimPreRunning`; the CODE-2 heading; the race item about `terminalReclaimPreRunning`). Those are correct; do not "finish" the rename there. Only the claim-predicate sense moved to publication.
DECISION: recorded the pre-projection delete as a spec-changes **Edge cases** departure ("A release delete before the pod projects `claimed` (departure).") and replaced the §5.2 Site-classification "rows D1, D3c, D14, and D15 agree with it" with a pointer to Edge cases — BECAUSE the gap predates 0093 (no release writes `failed`; release.go sends dispositionFailed to the delete path) and the tenant label bounds it (pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go no-claim switch: `reserved` → idle, warm-fill `idle` untouched) — ALTERNATIVES: see WATCHOUT below.
WATCHOUT: do not restore the SPEC-2d `failed` write for failed/crashed mcs1 sessions, or make every no-reuse release write `failed`, or have the controller drain on a reserved/idle-level delete. The first covers only one subset of the window; the second is a new rule extending claim lifetime to pod termination (candidate follow-on if the owner wants scrub-before-idle fail-closed); the third cannot tell a release delete from a hold expiry and would retire every hold-expired pod.
FACT: on a non-recycling mcs1 pool the departure is still bounded: claimIdle stamps `lenny.dev/tenant-id`, and §5.2 **Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions).** makes every acquisition outside the rule refuse a labeled pod and stamp its drain request. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md "Deployer acknowledgment" paragraph ("A pod has served an earlier session when it is `reserved` or its `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label is set").

DECISION: SPEC-2c's slot clause keys on gateway publication ("when no gateway replica published the session's binding to the pod", citing §7.3), and the non-spec table's Slot column becomes a Binding column (`unpublished`/`published`) — BECAUSE §6.2/§4.7.1 put `running` at the adapter's record, which precedes `registerBinding` and which the gateway cannot observe when a start RPC's outcome is unknown; every row's existing classification already meant "published", so this is a rename and CODE-2 item 7 stays — ALTERNATIVES: keep §6.2 `running` and reclassify D18b/D14/D6a ReleaseUncommitted as `running` (rejected: ReleaseUncommitted and Launch-failure rows would then need recycle code, and the gateway cannot evaluate the adapter record); key on the session's `running` commit (rejected: diverges from the tree when `registerBinding` succeeds and `commitStart` is superseded, where the hook's executor release recycles on clean).
FACT: the tree's binding publication is `publishBinding` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_registration.go`), called by `registerBinding` on `/start` and directly on the hoisted early-commit paths (create_and_start.go, delegated_child_materialize.go) and the resume driver after the `resuming → running` commit (resume_driver.go). The only spec use of the phrase is §7.3 **Exits from `resuming`.** ("named in no session-row field and no published binding"). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_registration.go:14-60, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443
DECISION: Finding 2 (a release delete that lands before the controller projects `claimed`) is recorded as a spec-changes Edge-cases departure, and the §5.2 Site-classification entry cites it — BECAUSE the tenant boundary holds (the pod keeps its tenant label; §5.2 Tenant pinning and Deployer acknowledgment confine reuse to the pinned tenant, and a pool that admits no served pod refuses and drain-stamps it), the gap predates 0093 (release.go deletes on `dispositionFailed`; nothing writes `failed`), and the owner prefers bounded departures over new rules — ALTERNATIVES: restore the `failed` write for failed/crashed mcs1 (rejected: closes only that case, not pre-running or first-acquisition deletes, and has no code); the gateway writes `failed` instead of every release delete (rejected: rewrites every delete row and the claim lifetime, a new rule); the controller drains any claim delete from `idle`/`reserved` (rejected: indistinguishable from hold expiry and the acquisition-path hold end); the gateway waits for the `claimed` projection after a rebind (rejected: new wait mechanism and latency).
FACT: the window is wider than the `reserved` rebind. A claim deleted while the pod still projects `idle` (fresh acquisition, before the controller projected `claimed`) leaves the pod `idle`, because the projection owns no edge from a warm-fill phase. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:104-131, spec/04_system-components.md:411,415-416

A delete before that projection returns the pod to `idle` unscrubbed (the Edge-cases departure this round adds).

### [spec.3.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The occupancy projection leaves a pod's phase untouched when no claim exists and the pod's last projected level is a warm-fill phase (idle), so a claim created and deleted between two reconciles of the OccupancyReconciler (for example during the up-to-25s WarmPoolController leader failover) never drives the pod to `claimed`, and the pod stays `idle` with whatever the pre-running bind did to it. This is a level-triggered gap in the §4.6.1 projection that predates 0093. 0093 no longer states a pod outcome at any releasing site (SPEC-2f, SPEC-2j, SPEC-2k), so it is not a finding against this proposal. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:84-140 (the `default: return "", false` arm for no claim on an idle pod); spec/04_system-components.md:409-416
DECISION: Returned no findings for the Kubernetes-idiom lens in round 3 — BECAUSE SPEC-2c keeps the gateway as the sole SandboxClaim spec/status writer and deleter alongside the WarmPoolController's termination and GC deletes, which matches the §4.6.3 RBAC grants (`create`/`get`/`delete` on claims, `get`/`patch` on `sandboxclaims/status`). The staged edits add no status field used as a command inbox, no finalizer, no new apiserver verb, and no controller dependency on a synchronous path. SPEC-2d only narrows the `failed` enum meaning, and no WarmPoolController code keys a crashed-session behaviour on claim `failed` (`pod_retirement_meter.go` carries only `uptime_limit`). SPEC-2j, SPEC-2k, and SPEC-2l state no pod phase and cite §4.6.3 — ALTERNATIVES: filing the create-delete projection gap above (rejected because it is pre-existing and its owner is §4.6.1, which the SPEC-2 Site classification records as out of scope); filing the sibling-reservation TOCTOU on the at-zero delete (rejected because non-spec **Races the table does not close** now files it through REC-1 item 6).
USEFUL [spec.2.review-kubernetes.1]: the projection FACT (delete from `claimed` drains, from `reserved` returns to idle, and an unbound claim leaves the pod alone) saved re-deriving occupancy.go.

### [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: §6.2 `running` is an adapter-side fact. The slot reaches `running` at the adapter's record under the §4.7.1 registry critical section (rule 8, inside StartSession or another request that starts a session). The gateway's pod-registry Put (`registerBinding`/`publishBinding`) happens later, after `launchOnPod` returns. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:739 ("the record is the adapter's recording ... and the slot reaches [§6.2] `running` at that record"); spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:162; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:72,91-92; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:392
WATCHOUT: the disposition table's Slot column ("`running` once its binding passed the §4.7.1 record step, which is the pod-registry `Put` in `registerBinding`") and invariant 3 equate two different events. Rows D18/D18b (rollbackBinding of "a successful startOnPod result", after StartSession succeeded) and D14's ReleaseUncommitted (after a successful Resume) release a slot that §6.2 calls `running`. SPEC-2c cites §6.2 for "pre-`running`", so the staged spec requires `recycling` where CODE-2 item 7 and D14 delete. Filed as a spec finding. The spec-side fix is to key SPEC-2c on the gateway-observable fact (a binding never published), which matches the table and fails closed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:30,59-60,66,149; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_rollback.go:13; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:400-420
DECISION: CODE-2 item 7 (judged as the caller directed). It is correct only if SPEC-2c's pre-`running` clause is rekeyed to the published binding. Under the current SPEC-2c it contradicts SPEC-2e, because today's tree (`Binder.ReleaseSlot` passes `result.Recycle`) already matches the staged spec on D18b. BECAUSE StartSession succeeded before rollbackBinding runs. ALTERNATIVES: dropping item 7 and reclassifying D18/D18b/D14 as `running` (rejected as the preferred remedy: it needs new recycle code on the exclusive ReleaseUncommitted path and fails open relative to today).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, disposition table Slot column definition and invariant 3]: whichever way the finding is fixed, "the §4.7.1 record step, which is the pod-registry `Put` in `registerBinding`" misattributes the record step. §4.7.1's record is the adapter's. The column must name the gateway fact it actually uses (binding registered or published on a gateway replica) and cite SPEC-2c's wording, not §4.7.1.
FACT: SPEC-2j, SPEC-2k, and SPEC-2l (judged as the caller directed) each quote anchors that match spec/29 lines 725-727 and 772-774 and spec/07 line 221 verbatim. None of them states a pod outcome beyond a pointer to §4.6.1. Step 17 ("On the recycle disposition ...") stays unqualified, but step 12 defines the recycle disposition in the same trace with the §4.6.3 exception, so the two steps do not drift.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2 **Site classification.** says every site that states a claim or pod outcome at the end of an occupancy is listed. Three sites state the whole-pod scrub trigger as "occupancy reaches zero on a recycling pool" with no §4.6.3 exception and are not listed: the §7.1 `scrubPolicy` row (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:74) and the §6.1 table row for `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` with recycle (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:74). The third, the §4.7 `ReportPodScrub` row, is listed. These sites are the same descriptive class the classification already puts out of scope, so no finding was filed. A later single-source or edit-sites pass may want them listed.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3a step 1 now returns each lease to its pool before step 2's release tears down the session. For direct-delivery leases, the pod still holds the materialized key until its slot cleanup, so the per-credential concurrency count drops before the key leaves the pod. This is narrow and was not filed. P5b owns deny-list and lease granularity.
USEFUL [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]: the FACT that a pod-registry binding exists only from registration onward was the lead. This pass found that the record step it names is the adapter's, not the registry Put.

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the staged spec edits under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the set primitive keeps O(1) per-pod Lua ops at the shipped per-session rate (one reserve, one release), the per-pod key adds no hot key or cross-pod serialization, release-path rehydration keeps the at-most-one GetActiveSlotsByPod per pod per Redis restart bound that the §5.2 burst-mitigation paragraph quantifies, SPEC-2 adds no claim or status write rate beyond what the shipped code already does (the shipped exclusive pre-running reclaim and the concurrent ReleaseSlotReservation already delete), and every Redis-outage, Redis-restart, Postgres-failover, and coordinator-handoff path either matches the shipped behavior or improves it (keyed release is idempotent across replicas) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-credential over-subscription window in direct delivery mode (SPEC-3a step 1 returns pool slots before the revoked pods stop holding the raw key) was rejected as a bounded capacity effect on a rare admin action, against a shipped design that stranded the slot instead.
FACT: Counter.Reset has no production caller, so the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset that SPEC-1f rewords is not implemented in code — EVIDENCE: grep of pkg and cmd for `.Reset(ctx` finds only unrelated callers; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482
FACT: an empty Redis set is deleted when SREM removes its last member, so the set design leaves less key residue than the shipped string counter, which kept "0" with no TTL after the last DECR — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:222-228 (releaseScript DECR)
FACT: slot reserve and release ops are not in the §12.4 Tier 3 Redis write-throughput table. At 200 new sessions/s this is about 400 Lua calls/s on pod-scoped keys whether the key holds a counter or a set — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:259-268
WATCHOUT: a Redis outage with no data loss makes every concurrent-pool release in the window fail (bounded retry of about 5 s, slotCleanupFloor). Each failure leaves a ghost member that holds one slot until a Redis restart or pod retirement, because clearOutage never re-arms rehydration. The shipped counter behaves the same way. The §12.4 sentence "On Redis recovery, the slot set is rebuilt" is spec intent that the code does not implement. Remedy it in code or a REC-1 departure, not in a spec edit — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:138-145; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:433
USEFUL [spec.2.review-performance.1]: the Postgres-backed GetByToken FACT and the Sweeper 15 s fallback FACT settled the full_revoke durability question without re-deriving it.

### [spec.3.review-security.1]
FACT: The occupancy projection picks the edge for a deleted claim from Sandbox.status.phase (o.Current), the controller's last written level, and not from the binding state the claim last carried. No claim + Current reserved -> idle; no claim + Current claimed -> draining; no claim + any other Current -> no change. A `failed`/`released` binding drains regardless of Current. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:84-140; spec/04_system-components.md:415-416
FACT: The gateway does not wait for the projection after a `reserved -> bound` rebind (WriteRebindStatus) and the release deletes the claim by name with no precondition (Binder.ReclaimClaimed -> deleteClaimDetached). So a rebind followed by a delete that the controller observes coalesced (or after a leader failover) projects reserved -> idle for a pod that a session touched. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:302-316; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:79-113
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-security.1 UNVERIFIED rebind race]: verified real in code. It is pre-existing in code, but not in the spec on recycling pools: the pre-0093 spec wrote `failed` for a failed or crashed mcs 1 session (projection drains regardless of the last level), and SPEC-2d replaces that write with a delete whose outcome depends on the last level. Filed this round as a scrub-before-idle departure that the staging leaves unrecorded.
WATCHOUT: the site-classification line "table rows D1, D3c, D14, and D15 agree with it" (§5.2 retirement policy) and "Every mcs 1 non-recycling row deletes a claim the pod projects claimed under" both assume that the controller has already projected `claimed`. Neither holds while the controller lags the gateway, for example during a 25s leader failover. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:174-175
DECISION: did not re-file the C3 credential-granular deny list or the return-to-pool of a deny-listed pool credential in SPEC-3a step 1 — BECAUSE the owner moved it to P5b (directive) — ALTERNATIVES: none.

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c defines binding publication in place with one appositive ("the gateway step that follows the pod's acceptance of the session's start or resume and from which the session's message delivery and its release reach the pod") and keeps the predicate wording and the §7.3 citation verbatim — BECAUSE spec/ defines publication only for the resume replacement pod (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md **Exits from `resuming`.**), and every disposition-table row already classifies by the tree's publication step, so defining it changes no row — ALTERNATIVES: rekey on the running commit (flips D3b/D4b, needs new code); rekey on §6.2/§4.7.1 running (round 3 rejected it); key on pod_assignment (create.go writes it at claim time); a separate §10.1.1/§7.3 definition (second anchor for a one-use term); naming the lease holder (does not affect the predicate).

FACT: the tree publishes in three ways: `registerBinding` (lease acquire, then `publishBinding`) on /start before `commitStart`; `publishBinding` directly after the early running-commit on create-and-start and delegated-child materialize, with no failure point between commit and publish; and the resume tail after the `resuming → running` commit, through `publishBinding` on the rebuild branch and a bare `podRegistry.Put` on the checkpoint branch. ### [spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: The tenant pin is stamped on every pool kind at every acquisition, so a pod that the new "release delete before the pod projects claimed" departure returns to idle always carries lenny.dev/tenant-id; on a pool outside the kept-runtime rule every acquisition refuses it and stamps a drain request, so only a recycling kept-runtime pool lets the pinned tenant reacquire it unscrubbed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:228 (StampPodTenant after writeBoundStatus), pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go:149, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:559 ("A pod has served an earlier session when it is `reserved` or its `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label is set")
FACT: The served-session count that bounds recycle.maxSessionsPerPod is Postgres-backed (agent_pod_state.sessions_served), incremented by the gateway on ReportSessionScrub, so a claim delete does not reset it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/agentpodstate/pgstore/pgstore.go:330-343
DECISION: Filed no finding on the SPEC-2c predicate change (pre-running slot to unpublished binding) — BECAUSE either misclassification direction lands on a scrub (recycling) or a delete that drains a pod projecting claimed, both fail-closed; the only unscrubbed-reuse window is the projection-lag departure the previous round recorded — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the projection-independent `failed` write removal (SPEC-2d) was rejected as re-litigation of a fixed round-3 finding.
OPEN: "published the session's binding" has no spec definition for the /start path; SPEC-2c cites §7.3, which uses the term only for the resume replacement pod (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443). Not a security defect (both directions fail closed); a citation-lens question.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the departure bullet "A release delete before the pod projects `claimed` (departure)." stops describing what later acquisitions do to the pod. Its tenant-pin sentence becomes the classification this site alone knows (the pod keeps its `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label, so it is a §5.2 pinned idle pod) followed by a pointer to §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** and **Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions).** — BECAUSE rounds 3, 4, and 5 each restated §5.2's admission/refusal/retirement rule and each restatement was narrower and still wrong (round 3: "only its pinned tenant"; round 4: "every acquisition refuses it and retires it"; round 5: the fallback claim stamps no drain request). The reviewer's suggested wording is the same restatement one step narrower and would be next round's finding (§5.2 also has the failed-stamp retry, the failed-pin-read path, and controller-side pinned-inventory accounting). — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "the first acquisition that reads it from its Sandbox retires it" (rejected: degree change of a failed restatement; a failed stamp does not retire, so it is still false); deleting the sentence outright (rejected: the label fact is what bounds the departure for a security reader, and §5.2 does not say a release delete leaves the label).
FACT: the tenant label survives the claim delete: `lenny.dev/tenant-id` is admission-immutable (pkg/admission/label_immutability, imported by pkg/controller/warmpool/pinned.go), and `markPinnedIdle` treats an idle labelled pod with no `claim-<name>` SandboxClaim as pinned. A pod whose claim the release deletes before projection matches spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** exactly. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pinned.go `markPinnedIdle`; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:449
WATCHOUT: do not restate in this bullet which acquisition admits, refuses, or retires a pinned pod. §5.2 **Deployer acknowledgment** distinguishes Sandbox-reading acquisitions (stamp drain-request) from the Postgres-backed fallback claim (no stamp), and a failed stamp defers retirement to the next refusing acquisition. Any sentence here that summarizes that is a second home and has failed three rounds running. A tier-8 departure test asserts what §5.2 states, cited by heading. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:559
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-G3.1]: "every other pool refuses and retires the pod" is false for the Postgres-backed fallback claim (refuses, stamps no drain request) and for a failed stamp. Its companion WATCHOUT (tier-8 test asserts refusal plus drain-request and retirement) holds only for a Sandbox-reading acquisition whose stamp succeeds.
USEFUL [spec.4.review-security.1]: its FACT that `StampPodTenant` runs on every acquisition settled that the label is always present on this departure.

### [spec.7.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings in the staged spec edits at round 7 — BECAUSE the set keeps one Lua round trip per reserve and per release (about 200/s at Tier 3, against the ~6,500/s §12.4 Redis budget), the release-path rehydration stays at one GetActiveSlotsByPod query per pod per Redis restart under the existing SET NX lock, and SPEC-2c deletes where the tree already deletes, so it adds no etcd writes to the §4.6.1 Tier 3 estimate — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Sentinel-failover lost-SADD case (rejected: the lost member leaves at most a GC-bounded stranded claim, where the shipped clamp-at-zero counter runs the occupancy-zero disposition under a live sibling, so the set design degrades less).
FACT: `slotcounter.Counter.Reset` has no caller in pkg/ or cmd/, so neither the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset nor a pod retirement clears `active_slots` or `rehydrated` today. A `leaked:` member or a `rehydrated` flag outlives its pod, as the shipped counter key already does — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482 (the only hit for Reset).
FACT: the rehydration query uses the 0080 partial index (`pod_assignment <> ''`, non-terminal), and the predicate matches the code's NOT IN list. Selecting `id` in place of `count(*)` costs at most maxConcurrentSessions heap rows — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:836-851; migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql:23-24.
WATCHOUT: a Redis outage that is not a restart leaves the `rehydrated` flag in place, so fallback-admitted sessions never enter the set after recovery. §12.4 says "On Redis recovery, the ... is rebuilt", and the code rebuilds only when the flag is absent. Both designs share this gap (it predates 0093), and the set design over-releases less than the counter — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:283-296.
UNVERIFIED: the Redis Cluster CROSSSLOT question for the two-key release script (`active_slots` plus `rehydrated` with no hash tag in code). reserveScript already uses two keys. This is code-only and belongs to the non-spec loop if anyone owns it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:238-252.

### [spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: The round-6 to round-7 diff is one line: the Edge cases bullet "A release delete before the pod projects `claimed` (departure)". It now states only the `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label fact and points to §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** and **Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions).**. Both definitions match the post-delete state: session-mode, `idle`, non-empty label, and no SandboxClaim. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:449, :559
FACT: The §5.2 whole-pod replacement trigger's leaked count is held in an in-memory per-replica tracker (`slothealth.Tracker.leaked`). It does not read the Redis slot set. The `leaked:<sessionId>` members SPEC-1a adds, and the reseed departure that drops them, therefore do not reset that residual-state bound. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/slothealth/slothealth.go:1-17, :55-66
FACT: The adapter does not read `USER_REVOKED`, and Decision 9 records that. Dropping the reason in SPEC-3a step 2 removes no audit or alert surface. A grep across spec/, schemas/, and docs/ finds the reason only at the §11.4 step-2 sentence that SPEC-3a replaces. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:271; summary.md Decision 9
DECISION: Filed no security finding in round 7. BECAUSE: (1) SPEC-2c's delete-versus-recycle split fails closed in both misclassification directions, and the projection-lag window is a recorded departure. (2) Removing the `failed` write (SPEC-2d) drops no metric or alert, because `lenny_gateway_pod_retirement_total` carries no session-failure reason. (3) SPEC-3a deny-lists before the first cancel, which is stricter than before. — ALTERNATIVES: I rejected filing three issues. The deny-list sweep could remove an entry once step 1 has returned every lease. A returned pool lease could be deny-listed in the same step. Both are part of C3 and Decision 18, which P5b owns (summary.md:96 and :111). The Redis-outage release, which cannot run the set script and so leaves the claim to the orphan GC, fails safe and predates this proposal.
